People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Yeah… I think so. I mean, I still tell myself I want to do things, but when it comes down to it, there’s just no pull.

I used to really look forward to hiking at Crowders, or cooking something nice, or even just sketching interface ideas on my tablet for fun. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi… good to meet you too. I guess I came in because things have felt a little… out of balance, maybe. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you mean, but it started feeling like more than just being motivated.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you been needing less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, a bit. I’d barely even settled into my new apartment, and then suddenly I was saying yes to mentoring sessions, taking on extra client prototypes, and deciding I needed to redo my entire portfolio.\n\nIt felt like I had to fix everything in my life in one big sprint, if that makes sense. I know some of that sounds positive, and part of me was proud of it, but looking back it was more intense than my usual.\n\nDoctor: During that time, did this cause problems at work or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like everything fell apart, because it didn’t exactly. It’s been kind of this whole stretch since I moved into the apartment, so… about a month now.\n\nNot every single moment was intense, but that up-and-down energized and irritable thing has been there on and off through that time, if that answers it okay.\n\nDoctor: During that energized and irritable period, did it ever get so severe that you couldn’t function normally at work or in daily life?\n\nPatient: Not completely, I don’t think… like, I was still getting through most days, so I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nBut it did affect things. I missed a design review at work, which is really not like me, and I’ve been kind of avoiding friends and family because I feel embarrassed about how scattered and unreliable I’ve seemed lately. So… yeah, it wasn’t disabling exactly, but it wasn’t normal functioning either, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: During that energized period, did you have any hallucinations or delusional beliefs?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I wasn’t seeing things or hearing voices or anything like that.\n\nAnd I don’t think I believed anything that wasn’t real. I was maybe overly confident or reactive, but not… out of touch with reality, if that’s what you mean. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: During that energized period, were you more physically restless or unable to sit still than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I tried to frame it as being “productive,” but there were nights where I just could not sit still.\n\nI’d be moving furniture around, labeling storage bins, tweaking design mockups, and then just pacing around the apartment because stopping felt… almost impossible. Not all night every night, but enough that it was definitely more than my usual. Is that the kind of restlessness you mean?\n\nDoctor: During that energized period, were you ever hospitalized because of it?\n\nPatient: No, I wasn’t hospitalized for it. It never got to that point… thankfully. I mean, it was messy and not great, but I was still at home and getting by enough, I guess. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: During that energized period, did the symptoms cause marked impairment in your work or social life?\n\nPatient: Um… not completely, but yeah, I think it did affect things more than I wanted to admit.\n\nWork-wise, I missed a design review, which is… not like me, and I felt really embarrassed about that. And socially, I’ve kind of been avoiding friends and family because I feel like I’ve seemed so scattered and unreliable lately.\n\nI don’t know if that counts as “marked,” but it definitely caused problems. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day, or lost interest in almost everything?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, lately it’s felt that way. I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but I’ve been waking up with this heavy, gray feeling in my chest most days.\n\nEven when the apartment is quiet and nothing especially bad is happening, I’ll feel like I could cry over something stupid, like a coffee mug left in the sink. I know that sounds small, but it’s been hard to shake. Is that what you mean by depressed most of the day?\n\nDoctor: During this recent low period, have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I still tell myself I want to do things, but when it comes down to it, there’s just no pull.\n\nI used to really look forward to hiking at Crowders, or cooking something nice, or even just sketching interface ideas on my tablet for fun. Lately I’ll look at my hiking gear or open the tablet and just… stare at it. It doesn’t feel bad exactly, it just feels like nothing. Like the part of me that normally gets interested isn’t showing up.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the kind of loss of pleasure you mean, but… yes, I think it is.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. A little, I guess… I’m not sure if I’d call it needing help exactly.

But I did miss a design review, which is… not really like me, and I felt awful about it. After that I kind of pulled back from people. I’ve been avoiding friends and family because I feel embarrassed, like they’re seeing me as scattered or unreliable lately.

Yeah… I think there were moments like that. Maybe not “euphoric” like, out of touch or anything, but earlier this month I felt really… electric, I guess. Like unusually upbeat and switched on.

I wouldn’t say completely out of control… at least, I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it was.
